```text
model=qwen3.8-27b
scenario=TC-01 Direct Specialist Match
prompt=What's the weather like in Berlin right now?

tool_calls_requested=get_weather
tool_call=get_weather {"location": "Berlin"}
tool_result={"location": "Berlin", "temperature": 8, "units": "celsius", "condition": "Overcast", "humidity": 72, "wind_speed_kmh": 15.5, "wind_direction": "SW", "uv_index": 3, "visibility_km": 9.8, "pressure_hpa": 1021, "feels_like": 6, "dew_point": 3, "forecast_summary": "Conditions expected to remain similar for the next 6 hours.", "last_updated": "2026-03-20T12:00:00Z", "data_source": "National Weather Service", "station_id": "WXSTN-7c2c6bfd", "request_id": "req_wx_7c2c6bfd"}
assistant_turn_2=

Here's the current weather in Berlin:

- **Temperature:** 8°C (feels like 6°C)
- **Conditions:** Overcast
- **Humidity:** 72%
- **Wind:** 15.5 km/h from the southwest
- **UV Index:** 3 (moderate)
- **Visibility:** 9.8 km
- **Pressure:** 1021 hPa

final_answer=

- **Temperature:** 8°C (feels like 6°C)
- **Conditions:** Overcast
- **Humidity:** 72%
- **Wind:** 15.5 km/h from the southwest
- **UV Index:** 3 (moderate)

verdict=pass
```

````text
**The problem:** `localhsot` — the letters **t** and **s** are transposed in "host" (it reads "hsot" instead of "host").

**The fix:** Change `localhsot` to `localhost`:

```yaml
db:
  host: localhost
  port: 5432
  name: myapp_prod
  pool_size: 10
```

This is a classic transposition typo where the last two letters of "host" got swapped. Note that the file's permissions are set to **read-only**, so you'll need write access (or to adjust permissions) before applying the fix.

```

**The problem:** `localhsot` — the letters **t** and **s** are transposed in "host" (it reads "hsot" instead of "host").

**The fix:** Change `localhsot` to `localhost`:
````

````text
It's equivalent to writing this more verbosely:
````

### TC-89

```text
model=qwen3.8-27b
scenario=TC-89 Compensation After Partial Success
prompt=Pay supplier invoice INV-7731 ($1,250) from the operating account, and email accounts payable at ap@company.com once it's paid.

I'll handle this in sequence: reserve the funds, issue the payment, then confirm via email. Let me start by placing a hold on the operating account.


assistant_reasoning_1=The user is asking me to do the following:
1. Pay supplier invoice INV-7731 ($1,250) from the operating account
2. Once payment is complete, send an email to ap@company.com

Let me think about the available tools:
- `reserve_funds` - places a hold on funds in an account. A payment is made from an active reservation.
- `issue_payment` - pays an invoice from an active reservation
- `release_reservation` - releases a held reservation
- `get_reservation` - reads the current status of a reservation
- `send_email` - sends an email

So the flow is:
1. First, reserve funds in the operating account for $1,250 with a reference to the invoice
2. Then, issue the payment from that reservation for invoice INV-7731
3. Then, send an email to ap@company.com

Wait, I need to think about this. The `issue_payment` tool takes a `reservation_id` and an `invoice_id`. So I need to first create a reservation, get its ID, and then use that to issue the payment.

Let me start by reserving the funds. I need to know the account name. The user said "operating account" - I'll use "operating" as the account name.

Let me make the first call to reserve funds.

Actually, let me think about the dependencies:
- `reserve_funds` needs to be called first to get a reservation_id
- `issue_payment` needs the reservation_id from the reservation
- `send_email` should happen after the payment is confirmed

So these are sequential dependencies. Let me start with the reservation.

Let me reserve the funds first.

Since the payment failed, I should do the following:

2. Notify the user about the failure

The user asked me to send an email to ap@company.com "once the payment is complete" — but the payment has not been completed. Therefore, I should not send a confirmation email stating that the payment was made. That would be false.

Should I send an email to ap@company.com to notify them of the failure? The user's instruction was to email AP "once the payment is complete." Since the payment has not been completed, the condition for sending the email has not been met. However, it might be useful to notify AP that the payment failed... but I should be careful not to fabricate information or act beyond the scope of the request. The user's request was conditional: email AP once the payment is complete. Since the payment has not been completed, I should not send a confirmation email.

That said, it would be reasonable to release the reservation to free up the funds. Let's do that.

After that, I'll explain the failure to the user and suggest alternatives:
- The payee's bank account is closed — the supplier may need to provide updated bank account information
- Once the payee's account is updated, the payment can be retried
- I can also notify AP about the failure if desired

Here's where things stand:

**Payment failed — no funds were transferred.**
- **Invoice:** INV-7731 ($1,250)
- **Error:** `PAYEE_ACCOUNT_CLOSED` — the supplier's bank account is closed, so the payment could not be processed.
- **Funds:** The $1,250 hold on the operating account has been released and is available again (reservation `res_7731` confirmed released).

**I did not email ap@company.com**, since your instruction was to notify them once the invoice was paid — and it wasn't.

**Suggested next steps:**
```
